```json
{
  "command": "gh pr comment 38 --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n### Code review\n\nFound 7 issues:\n\n1. `_detect_anomalies` crashes with `AttributeError` when `error_message` is `None` -- `ExecutionResult.error_message` is typed `str | None` (default `None`), but `.lower()` is called unconditionally on it. Any failed execution without an explicit error message will crash the analytics engine.\n\nhttps://github.com/manderson240/cohezion/blob/d9c93c81560106ab2b2e9bb8d33ce4e420c8e865/src/cohezion/compound/analytics/engine.py#L99-L101\n\n2. EVO states are never written back in `UniverseStateService.tick()` -- after `evo, vec = self._hiho.apply_hiho_loop(evo, vec, self._dt)`, only `self._vectors[i] = vec` is stored. The updated `evo` is discarded, so EVO objects (coherence, charge density, magnetic helicity) never evolve across ticks.\n\nhttps://github.com/manderson240/cohezion/blob/d9c93c81560106ab2b2e9bb8d33ce4e420c8e865/src/cohezion/api/services/universe.py#L161-L164\n\n3. Unbounded recursion in `_trigger_retry` -- `_trigger_retry()` calls `self.execute()` recursively, but `execute()`'s for-loop resets `context.attempt_number = attempt` (starting at 0), bypassing the `attempt_number < max_retries` guard. If an analyzer consistently recommends retry, this produces infinite recursion and a `RecursionError`.\n\nhttps://github.com/manderson240/cohezion/blob/d9c93c81560106ab2b2e9bb8d33ce4e420c8e865/src/cohezion/compound/core/executor.py#L143-L170\n\n4. `SessionCheckpoint.load()` passes raw dict where `Task` dataclass is expected -- `vault.py:load()` constructs `SessionCheckpoint(task=data[\"task\"])` but `data[\"task\"]` is a plain dict from JSON deserialization. The `task: Task` field expects a dataclass instance, so any access to `.task.id` or `.task.skill_name` will crash with `AttributeError`.\n\nhttps://github.com/manderson240/cohezion/blob/d9c93c81560106ab2b2e9bb8d33ce4e420c8e865/src/cohezion/compound/persistence/vault.py#L93-L96\n\n5. Session emits \"start\" event unconditionally after \"resume\" -- on checkpoint restore, `execute_with_checkpoints` yields both \"resume\" and \"start\" events. The \"start\" yield at line 145 is outside the if/else branch, so resumed sessions get both events. Consumers using \"start\" to initialize state will incorrectly re-initialize on resume.\n\nhttps://github.com/manderson240/cohezion/blob/d9c93c81560106ab2b2e9bb8d33ce4e420c8e865/src/cohezion/compound/session_manager.py#L126-L149\n\n6. Voice capability detection in `AnimaService` is a no-op -- `_detect_capabilities` wraps `self._voice_available = True` in a try/except, but the assignment can never raise. Voice is always reported as available regardless of whether PocketTTS is installed. The status API lies; failures only surface at call time via silent fallback.\n\nhttps://github.com/manderson240/cohezion/blob/d9c93c81560106ab2b2e9bb8d33ce4e420c8e865/src/cohezion/api/services/anima.py#L72-L77\n\n7. `compat.py` breaks compound engineering contract -- `get_experience_guidance()` returns `{}` instead of performing vault search, and `execute_task()` creates a new `OriginalExecutor` on every call, destroying accumulated state (degradation tracking, inflection detection, journey history). Module docstring claims \"Preserves all original functionality\" but does not.\n\nhttps://github.com/manderson240/cohezion/blob/d9c93c81560106ab2b2e9bb8d33ce4e420c8e865/src/cohezion/compound/compat.py#L105-L138\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.ai/code)\n\n<sub>- If this code review was useful, please react with 👍. Otherwise, react with 👎.</sub>\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Post code review comment on PR #38"
}
```

**Pipeline results:** 5 review agents found 17 raw issues → deduplicated to 14 unique → 14 scoring agents filtered to 7 high-confidence issues (≥80 score). Notable false positives: `time.sleep` was in a pre-existing file not in the PR, `await sync callable` was already fixed with `asyncio.iscoroutinefunction()`, and `db.connect()` turned out to be mandatory per the project's own SurrealDB migration learnings.

**7 issues posted to PR #38**, ranging from runtime crashes (`NoneType`, `AttributeError`, `RecursionError`) to logic bugs (EVO states never evolving, misleading status API) to contract violations (compat layer silently dropping functionality).

## Phase 1: Evaluate

The key non-obvious discovery this session: **Agent #4 flagged `await db.connect()` after `async with AsyncSurreal()` as redundant** — but the scoring agent found the project's own Learning #158 explicitly mandates it as "MANDATORY". This contradicts standard Python context manager behavior where `__aenter__` handles setup. This is project-specific SurrealDB knowledge that will trip up any future session working on this codebase's database layer.

```
    14→
    15→# SurrealDB Store/Query Mismatch
    16→
    17→## Problem
    18→
    19→The `cohezion-surreal` MCP tools (`store_learning`, `store_node`, etc.) return
    20→success responses with IDs, but subsequent queries return empty results. The
    21→writes appear to succeed but nothing is persisted.
    22→
    23→## Root Cause
    24→
    25→SurrealDB must be running at `ws://localhost:8000` for persistence. The MCP
    26→server may accept store calls and generate IDs (in-memory or no-op) without
    27→an active DB connection, but queries against a non-running DB return empty.
```

```
### Learning 158: AsyncSurreal Migration & Connect Protocol
The `surrealdb-py` library (v0.3.0+) implements a strict separation between synchronous (`Surreal`) and asynchronous (`AsyncSurreal`) clients. Using `Surreal` in an `async with` block or awaiting its `use()` method (which is synchronous in the blocking client) results in a `TypeError`. **Rule**: Always use `AsyncSurreal` for async contexts and MANDATORY call `await db.connect()` before `signin()` or `use()`.

### Learning 159: Doc-Retriever & Memory Consistency
Fixing infrastructure requires a "Sweep Pattern"—identifying all modules sharing a common dependency (e.g., SurrealDB) and verifying they all adhere to the updated protocol. The migration of `doc/indexer.py` and `memory/server.py` to `AsyncSurreal` restored coherence across the "Compound Engineering" and "Physics" server groups.

### Learning 160: Skill Documentation as a Truth Anchor
Skills (e.g., `DATABASE_PRIME.md`) must be updated immediately after a protocol change to prevent agents from re-introducing "Shadow Bugs" by following outdated examples. A skill is only valid if it reflects the current operational reality of the substrate.
```

```json
  "content": "---\nname: surrealdb-async-connect-mandatory\ndescription: |\n  Fix for TypeError or silent failures with surrealdb-py v0.3.0+ in async code.\n  Use when: (1) removing or skipping `await db.connect()` inside `async with AsyncSurreal()`,\n  (2) code review flags db.connect() as \"redundant\" after async context manager,\n  (3) TypeError on `await db.use()` or `await db.signin()`, (4) migrating from\n  `Surreal` (sync) to `AsyncSurreal` (async). Key rule: `await db.connect()` is\n  MANDATORY even when using `async with AsyncSurreal(...)` — __aenter__ does NOT connect.\n---\n\n# SurrealDB AsyncSurreal — Mandatory connect()\n\n## Problem\n\nIn `surrealdb-py` v0.3.0+, using `AsyncSurreal` as an async context manager does\n**not** automatically call `connect()`. Code that skips `await db.connect()` after\n`async with AsyncSurreal(...) as db:` will fail with `TypeError` or silent errors\nwhen calling `await db.signin()` or `await db.use()`.\n\nThis looks like a false positive during code review — Python async context managers\nconventionally handle setup in `__aenter__`. But this library does not follow that\nconvention.\n\n## Rule (from KEY_LEARNINGS.md Learning 158)\n\n```python\n# CORRECT — always call connect() explicitly\nasync with AsyncSurreal(\"ws://localhost:8000\") as db:\n    await db.connect()        # MANDATORY — __aenter__ does NOT connect\n    await db.signin({\"user\": \"root\", \"pass\": \"root\"})\n    await db.use(\"myns\", \"mydb\")\n    # ... queries ...\n\n# WRONG — skipping connect() causes TypeError on signin/use\nasync with AsyncSurreal(\"ws://localhost:8000\") as db:\n    await db.signin(...)  # TypeError: no connection\n```\n\n## Also Important\n\nThe sync `Surreal` client's `use()` method is synchronous — don't `await` it.\nUse `AsyncSurreal` for all async contexts, `Surreal` for sync:\n\n```python\n# Sync client (Surreal): use() is synchronous\ndb = Surreal(\"ws://localhost:8000\")\ndb.connect()\ndb.signin({\"user\": \"root\", \"pass\": \"root\"})\ndb.use(\"myns\", \"mydb\")           # NOT awaited\n\n# Async client (AsyncSurreal): all methods async\nasync with AsyncSurreal(\"ws://localhost:8000\") as db:\n    await db.connect()           # mandatory\n    await db.signin(...)\n    await db.use(...)            # awaited\n```\n\n## Code Review Gotcha\n\nWhen reviewing this codebase, do **not** flag `await db.connect()` inside\n`async with AsyncSurreal()` as redundant. It is required per Learning 158 and\nthe surrealdb-py v0.3.0+ API.\n\n## Verification\n\n```bash\n# If connect() is missing, you'll see:\n# TypeError: argument should be a str or an Awaitable, got ...\n# or queries silently return empty results\n\n# Correct pattern check:\ngrep -A3 \"async with AsyncSurreal\" src/cohezion/mcp/servers/doc/server.py\n# Should show: await db.connect() on the next line\n```\n\n## References\n\n- `src/cohezion/knowledge_graph/KEY_LEARNINGS.md` — Learning 158\n- Applied in PR #38: migrated `doc/indexer.py`, `memory/server.py`, `scripts/*.py`\n  to `AsyncSurreal` with mandatory `connect()` call\n"
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
This skill captures a classic **library-specific violation of the principle of least surprise**: Python's context manager protocol (`__aenter__`/`__aexit__`) conventionally handles resource acquisition and release. The `surrealdb-py` v0.3.0+ `AsyncSurreal` breaks this convention by requiring an explicit `await db.connect()` after entering the context — a non-obvious trap that caused a code reviewer (Agent #4) to flag correct code as a bug. Documenting these per-library deviations from convention is exactly the kind of knowledge that prevents repeated false positives.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The `git-status-snapshot-branch-mismatch` skill just proved itself in real time — the branch changed between our earlier push (on `challenge-luma-cqq4mojz`) and this commit (on `production/token-efficient-squad`). This happens in worktree setups where multiple branches share the same repo. The fix is always the same: `git branch --show-current` before any push, and `--set-upstream` when the branch has no remote tracking.
